We can implement an optimizer with the following per iteration:

- grad = 2*correlate(p, f, 'valid')

Adaptive lag weights:
```
def lag_weights(c, mode, tau):
  if mode=='hard_active': active = c >= threshold (e.g., M*(1-eps) or top q); p=1/active_sum else.
  elif 'power': p=(c/M)^beta; normalize.
  elif 'softmax': exp(c/T)
```

A more systematic optimization: Use binary search on λ and solve feasibility via iterative "barrier". For a given λ slightly below current M, try to find f with c≤λ. We can formulate as minimize ∑ max(0, c_k-λ)^2 (violation) + small KL? Use gradient descent on log f for violation. This can directly target a specific λ. Starting from baseline, try λ decreasing; if algorithm can find feasible low violation, accept. The smooth violation objective V(λ)=∑(c-λ)_+^2. Its gradient uses p=2(c-λ)_+ (normalize). This is similar to excess weights. We can run many gradient steps until V small. Then decrease λ. This is like projected subgradient for convex perfor. Could be effective. We can set λ sequence from M*(1-1e-4) down to target/ (maybe 1.5). Each phase with temperature. Since sum f=1 maintained.

Pseudo:
```
def violation_optimize(f, lam, deadline):
  logf=log(f)
  for it:
    c=conv; excess=np.maximum(c-lam,0)
    if excess.sum()<tol: break
    p=excess; p/=p.sum()
    grad=2*correlate(p,f,'valid')
    # mirror step with eta maybe 2
    update...
```
If λ infeasible, V may not go to zero; we can perturb. Then lower λ gradually. This focuses on exceeding target, not all peaks. Could achieve 1.5027 if feasible? Likely.

But the objective needs minimize max, not just current λ. If local infeasible, algorithm may cycle. We can use combinations: Use λ = current_best_score/(2n) - Δ, with Δ small. Run until no reduction in violation or timeout, then lower Δ. This is like sequential convex constraints.

We can also use "active set LP" analytic: Given λ, constraints c_k≤λ. The violation gradient may be weak if many constraints. But okay.

At optimum maybe no move.

Let's estimate effect of multiplicative update: If p concentrated on one peak lag k, grad_i=2a_{k-i}. Update reduces high a at positions paired with lag, increases others. This is like shifting mass to avoid that peak. Good.

Temperature scheduling: For violation λ, p=excess. If many lags exceed λ by tiny amount, p roughly flat over active, gradient tends to reduce mass more uniformly. Good.

Potential issue: p nonnegative, grad nonnegative; mirror update only decreases relative to positive grad, but normalizes so some coordinates increase. If grad=0 for some coordinates, they increase a lot for large eta. This can create spikes and new peaks. Line search controls.

We can also add a small entropy regularization to keep f smooth: p_smooth = p * (f? no) or add `eps` uniform to p to avoid zero gradient? Maybe not.

Alternative update: Instead of pure multiplicative, use "exponential family" update with learning rate η and additive noise.

Let's draft core functions:
```
def _autocorr(f, size=None):
    f=np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n=len(f)
    if size is None: size = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:2*n-1]
    # numerical negative tiny? max positive. For gradients, c may have tiny negatives; clip? autocorr should nonnegative but roundoff. Use np.maximum(c,0) for weights.
    return c
```
Use `np.convolve` for small? FFT okay.

`_score_f(f)`:
```
s=f.sum()
if s<1e-10 return inf
c=_autocorr(f)
return 2*len(f)*c.max()/(s*s)
```
But if f not normalized, cmax scales. For normalized sum1, use cmax.

`_normalize(f)`: f=np.maximum(f,0); s=f.sum(); if s<=0 return ones/n; return f/s.

`_log_normalize(logf)`: z=max; z=z-log; s=sum? Actually if logf includes log unnormalized, we produce f=np.exp(z); f/=f.sum(); logf=np.log(f) or adjust z - log s. We'll keep f and logf separate. To update: 
```
def _mirror_step(f, logf, grad, eta):
    gmax=np.max(grad)
    if gmax <= 0: return f, logf
    z = logf - eta*grad/gmax
    z -= np.max(z)
    newf=np.exp(z)
    s=newf.sum()
    if s < 1e-300: return f, logf
    newf /= s
    # logf from newf, with floor
    logf = np.log(newf + 1e-300)
    return newf, logf
```
Since we normalize each step, no drift. Could avoid logf and compute `np.log(f)` each time (avoid stale). E simpler and robust: `z = np.log(f + floor) - eta*grad/gmax`. O(n) log each iteration. Acceptable? log 6k*200k=1.2B maybe heavy but okay? Maybe maintain logf. But due to normalization, logf can be updated as `logf_new = np.log(newf)`. Simpler, less bug. Use `f_floor=1e-300`. If f has zeros, log floor. Updates may significantly change zeros if eta*grad/gmax large? If logf=-30, z=-30, exp=9e-14; after normalization negligible. To revive zeros, noise needed.

def _weights_from_excess(c, lam, floor=0.0):
    excess = c - lam
    excess = np.maximum(excess,0)
    s=excess.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    return excess/s
```

`_mirror_block(f, deadline, rng, max_time, verbose)`:
- normalize f; c=auto; M=score/(2n); best...
- Initialize `eta=1.0`, `gap=0.05`, `fail=0`, `stuck=0`.
- For iter until deadline or max_iter ~? use while time.
- Determine weights p:
  - With probability 0.7: excess over threshold `M*(1-gap)`. If excess sum too small (<1e-10), use top q where q = int(np.max(10, 0.05*n))? Actually top 5% =306. Use p top q.
  - With prob 0.2: softmax T = M * random log-uniform [0.005,0.1]
  - With prob 0.1: top q random [20, 100, 300]
- grad = 2*correlate(p, f, 'valid')
- gmax=max(grad); if tiny break.
- f_try = normalize exp(logf - eta*grad/gmax)
- M_try = autocorr(f_try).max()
- If M_try < M*(1 - 1e-9): accept; update f, M, best; fail=0; eta = min(5, eta*1.05) if big improvement? Maybe keep.
  - if M_improve > 1e-5*M: gap = max(0.002, gap*0.7) (narrow active)
  - if small: gap *=1.1?
- Else: fail++; eta *=0.7; if fail>8: random perturb logf += rng.normal(0, 0.02, n) (maybe 0.05), renormalize; reset fail, eta=1, gap=random [0.01,0.2]; stuck++; if stuck>3: break or change strategy.
- To avoid using log of f after perturb, recompute logf = log(f+1e-300).
- Track best within block; return best.
```
This is heuristic. But if we also have LP, mirror may be redundant. Yet useful for final polish.

LP direction function details and fallback:
```
def _lp_direction(f, q):
    n=len(f); c=_autocorr(f); M=c.max()
    if q >= 2*n-1: q=2*n-1
    # select top q with random tie
    idx=np.argpartition(c, -q)[-q:]
    # optional ensure includes peaks with c> M-eps
    # Build K
    K=np.empty((q,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for a,k in enumerate(idx):
       j0=max(0,k-(n-1)); j1=min(n-1,k)
       js=np.arange(j0,j1+1)
       K[a, j0:j1+1] = f[k-js]
       # Need zero elsewhere; use zeros not empty
```

Line search `_best_mix(f, g, M_current)`:
```
def _best_mix(f,g,M):
  best_M=M; best_f=None
  for alpha in (0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5):
    f_try=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*g
    f_try=np.maximum(f_try,0)
    s=f_try.sum(); if s<1e-10: continue
    f_try/=s
    c_try=_autocorr(f_try); M_try=c_try.max()
    if M_try < best_M:
       best_M=M_try; best_f=f_try
  return best_f,best_M
```

def _make_starts(baseline, n, rng):
   out=[]
   def add(f): out.append(normalize(f))
   add(baseline)
   add(rev)
   add(sym)
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   for A in [0.5,1,2,5,10,20,50]:
      f=1 + A*(2*x-1)**2
      add
   for A in [0.5,2,10]:
      f=1/(1+A*(x-0.5)**2)
      add
   # Gaussian mixtures
   for centers in [(0.2,0.8),(0.3,0.7),(0.1,0.9)], widths in [0.05,0.1,0.2]:
       f=np.exp(-((x-c1)/w)^2)+np.exp(-((x-c2)/w)^2)+eps
       add
   # Random smooth: 3 frequencies
   for _ in range(20):
      f = 1
      for k in [2,4,6,8,10]:
         amp = 0.2*rng; phase = 2πrng
         f += amp*np.cos(2π k x + phase)
      f = np.maximum(f, 0)+1e-3
      add
   return out
```

`_grad_top(f, idx)`:
```
n=len(f); grad=np.zeros(n)
for k in idx:
  k=int(k)
  if k < n-1:
    grad[:k+1] += f[k::-1]
  else:
    left = k - (n-1)
    right = k
    if left == 0:
       grad[:right+1] += f[right::-1]
    else:
       grad[left:right+1] += f[right:left-1:-1]
return 2.0 * grad / len(idx)
```
Check if `right:left-1:-1` when left=0 would be f[right::-1]; handled. When left>0, length right-left+1. Good.

`_autocorr(f)`:
```
n=len(f)
size=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
F=np.fft.rfft(f, size)
c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:2*n-1]
# remove tiny negatives
if c.min() < 0:
   c = np.where(c<0, 0.0, c) # but allocating mask; use np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
return c
```
For gradient, c negatives not matter.

`_normalize(f)`:
```
f=np.asarray(f,dtype=np.float64).copy()
f=np.maximum(f,0)
s=f.sum()
if s <= 1e-12: return np.ones(len(f))/len(f)
f /= s
return f
```

`_score_cmax(cmax,n): return 2*n*cmax` (if normalized). For general f, use `_score(f)` computing sum.

`_cmax(f)`: c=_autocorr(f); return c.max()
But we often have c. Use.

LP direction function:
```
def _lp_direction(f, q, time_limit=5.0):
    n=len(f)
    c=_autocorr(f)
    M=float(c.max())
    if M <= 0: return None, None, None
    L=2*n-1
    q=int(min(q, L))
    # select top q; if q=L, all indices
    if q >= L:
        idx=np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx=np.argpartition(c, -q)[-q:]
        # sort? not needed
    b = c[idx]
    # Build scaled K
    # A = K/M shape (q,n)
    A = np.zeros((q,n), dtype=np.float64)
    invM=1.0/M
    for r,k in enumerate(idx):
        k=int(k)
        if k < n-1:
            A[r, :k+1] = f[k::-1] * invM
        else:
            left = k - (n - 1)
            if left == 0:
                A[r, :k+1] = f[k::-1] * invM
            else:
                right = k
                A[r, left:right+1] = f[right:left-1:-1] * invM
    # Now add t column coefficient -1
    A_ub = np.column_stack((A, -np.ones(q)))
    b_ub = b * invM
    # objective: min t - eps sum f_i g_i? Use eps=1e-8 maybe scaled? f_i 1e-4; contribution max 1e-12? Too small.
    cvec = np.zeros(n+1)
    cvec[-1] = 1.0
    # cvec[:n] = -1e-9 * f? maybe no
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
    bounds=[(0.0,None) for _ in range(n)] + [(-1.0,1.0)]
    try:
       res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=np.array([1.0]), bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': float(time_limit)})
    except Exception:
       return None, None, None
    if res is None or not res.success:
       return None, None, None
    g = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
    t = float(res.x[-1])
    g=np.maximum(g,0)
    s=g.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
       return None, None, None
    g/=s
    # if t >= -1e-10? maybe no first-order decrease; still return?
    # Add mild blending if extreme
    med = np.median(f) if n>0 else 0
    if med <=0: med=1e-6
    if g.max() > max(50*med, 5*f.max()+1e-12):
        # blend with f until max below bound or blend factor 0.75
        for blend in (0.5,0.3,0.2):
            g2 = (1.0-blend)*g + blend*f
            g2/=g2.sum()
            if g2.max() <= max(50*med, 5*f.max()+1e-12) or blend >=0.5:
                g=g2; break
    return g, t, (c, M)
```
Need note: For k< n-1, right=k, use f[k::-1], correct. For left=0, k=n-1, f[n-1::-1]. For left>0, `f[right:left-1:-1]`. If left-1 == -1? left=0 handled. If left>0, good. If left maybe n? k max 2n-2, left=n-1, right=2n-2, slice f[2n-2:n-2:-1] length2? okay.

def _best_mix(f, g, M):
   best_M=M; best_f=None
   for alpha in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.7):
       f_try = (1-alpha)*f + alpha*g
       np.maximum(f_try,0,out=f_try)
       s=f_try.sum()
       if s<1e-12: continue
       f_try /= s
       c_try=_autocorr(f_try)
       M_try=float(c_try.max())
       if M_try < best_M - 1e-15:
          best_M=M_try; best_f=f_try
   return best_f,best_M
```

Implement `_mirror_block(f, deadline, rng, max_q=120)`:
```
def _mirror_block(f, deadline, rng, max_q=120):
    f=_normalize(f)
    c=_autocorr(f); M=float(c.max())
    best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
    now=time.monotonic()
    if now>=deadline: return best_f,best_M
    eta=1.0
    fail=0
    q=50
    gaill=0
    logf=np.log(f+1e-300)
    iterations=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline-1:
        # choose q sometimes
        if fail > 6:
            q = int(min(max_q, max(20, q*1.5)))
        # top q indices
        L=2*len(f)-1
        qq=min(q,L)
        if qq>=L:
            idx=np.arange(L)
        else:
            idx=np.argpartition(c, -qq)[-qq:]
        grad=_grad_top(f, idx)
        gmax=float(grad.max())
        if gmax <= 1e-15:
            break
        # line search with current eta
        step=eta
        accepted=False
        for _ in range(6):
            z=logf - (step*grad/gmax)
            z -= z.max()
            f_try=np.exp(z)
            s=f_try.sum()
            if s < 1e-300:
                step*=0.5
                continue
            f_try /= s
            c_try=_autocorr(f_try)
            M_try=float(c_try.max())
            if M_try < M - 1e-14:
                # accept
                f=f_try; c=c_try; M=M_try; logf=np.log(f+1e-300)
                eta=min(2.0, step*1.2)
                fail=0
                accepted=True
                if M < best_M - 1e-15:
                    best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
                break
            else:
                step *= 0.5
                if step < 1e-4:
                    break
        if accepted:
            continue
        # no improvement
        fail +=1
        eta *= 0.6
        if fail > 12:
            # perturb best_f? current may be worse? current f is still previous because not accepted. We'll perturb best_f.
            sigma = 0.02 + 0.02*(fail//12)
            pert = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=len(f))
            f_try = best_f * np.exp(pert)
            f_try = np.maximum(f_try,0); s=f_try.sum(); f_try/=s
            f=f_try; c=_autocorr(f); M=float(c.max())
            logf=np.log(f+1e-300)
            fail=0; eta=1.0
            q=max(30, int(q*0.7))
            # If perturbation improved best? update if M<best_M
            if M < best_M:
                best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
        if fail > 30:
            # give up to caller
            break
        iterations +=1
    if M < best_M: best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
    return best_f,best_M
```
This line search accepts only if `M_try < M - 1e-14` (strict). It may be very slow with tiny improvements. Use tolerance `1e-15` maybe. For double precision, improvements of 1e-12 possible. Use `1e-12 * max(1,M)`? M~1e-4, tol 1e-16 absolute. Use `if M_try < M - 1e-16`. But to make progress, need allow tiny. We can set `tol=1e-15`. Good.

Potential: Direct top q gradient may not reduce M if top q includes many; line search step min eta small. If no improvement often, perturb. Good.

Main block loop:
```
def _improve_candidate(f0, deadline, rng):
    f=_normalize(f0); c=_autocorr(f); M=c.max()
    best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
    last_imp=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
       # if stagnation > 120s, perturb best and reset f
       if time.monotonic() - last_imp > 180:
           sigma=0.05
           f_try=best_f*np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,len(best_f)))
           f_try=_normalize(f_try)
           M_try=_autocorr(f_try).max()
           f=f_try; M=M_try
           # don't update best if worse
       # run LP block for up to 60s or until budget
       lp_end = min(time.monotonic()+60, deadline-20)
       f_lp, M_lp = _lp_block(f, lp_end, rng)
       if M_lp < best_M - 1e-14:
          best_f=f_lp.copy(); best_M=M_lp; last_imp=time.monotonic()
          f=f_lp.copy(); M=M_lp
       elif M_lp < M:
          f=f_lp.copy(); M=M_lp
       # if no improvement and time left, mirror block for 60s
       if time.monotonic() > deadline-20: break
       mir_end = min(time.monotonic()+90, deadline-20)
       f_mir, M_mir = _mirror_block(f, mir_end, rng)
       if M_mir < best_M - 1e-14:
          best_f=f_mir.copy(); best_M=M_mir; last_imp=time.monotonic()
          f=f_mir.copy(); M=M_mir
       elif M_mir < M:
          f=f_mir.copy(); M=M_mir
    return best_f,best_M
```
This will alternate LP/mirror. If LP block fails quickly, it returns current; then mirror. Good.

But `_lp_block` starts from f (maybe not best if perturbed). It keeps best within block. If no improve, returns input best maybe. Good.

Stagnation perturbation: if f is worse than best, LP/mirror may improve from perturbed; best remains. At end returns best. Good.

Now, let's reason potential bugs in LP matrix rows.

For f length n, convolution c_k = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} f_j f_{k-j}, where indices valid. The matrix K row for k: coefficient for variable g_j is f_{k-j}. We build A row `f[k-j]/M`. For k< n-1: valid j=0..k, values f[k], f[k-1], ..., f[0]. Slice `f[k::-1]` length k+1 assigned to columns 0..k. Good.
For k>=n-1: left=k-(n-1), right=k. valid j=left..right, values f[right], f[right-1],..., f[left]. If left=0 assign cols0..right (right=n-1) with f[right::-1] length right+1. If left>0 assign cols left..right with f[right:left-1:-1]. Test n=5,k=6,left=2,right=4: f[4:1:-1] gives f4,f3,f2 assigned cols2,3,4. Good.

def _make_starts(base, n, rng):
    base = _normalize(base)
    starts=[]
    starts.append(base.copy())
    rev = base[::-1].copy()
    starts.append(rev)
    sym = 0.5*(base+rev)
    starts.append(_normalize(sym))
    # perturb baseline
    for sigma in (0.01,0.02,0.04,0.08,0.15,0.25):
       f = base * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
       starts.append(_normalize(f))
    if n < 20000:
       x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
       for A in (0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0,10.0,20.0,50.0):
           f = 1.0 + A*(2.0*x-1.0)**2
           starts.append(_normalize(f))
       for A in (1.0,3.0,10.0,30.0):
           f = 1.0/(1.0 + A*(x-0.5)**2)
           starts.append(_normalize(f))
       for c1,c2 in ((0.2,0.8),(0.3,0.7),(0.1,0.9)):
          for w in (0.05,0.1,0.2):
             f=np.exp(-((x-c1)/w)**2)+np.exp(-((x-c2)/w)**2)+1e-6
             starts.append(_normalize(f))
       # random smooth
       for _ in range(10):
          f=np.ones(n)
          for k in (2,3,4,6,8,12):
             amp=0.1*rng.uniform(0.1,1.0)
             ph=2*np.pi*rng.random()
             f = f * (1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + ph))
          f = np.maximum(f,0.0)+1e-6
          starts.append(_normalize(f))
    # remove duplicates? Not necessary. But if many, evaluation time. Limit maybe len <= 60.
    if len(starts)>60:
       starts = starts[:60]
    return starts
```
Random smooth product may produce negative; take max. Good. But starts number: 3+6+7+4+9+10=39. Good.

Let's refine `_lp_block` to avoid infinite loops with LP solves taking entire block:
```
def _lp_block(f, deadline, rng):
   f=_normalize(f); c=_autocorr(f); M=float(c.max())
   best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
   q=100; fail=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
       if fail > 10: break
       remaining=deadline-time.monotonic()
       tlim = min(6.0, max(1.0, remaining*0.2))
       g,t = _lp_direction(f, q, tlim, rng)
       if g is not None and t < -1e-10:
           # blend
           blend=0.0
           if fail>=2: blend=min(0.5, 0.2+0.1*(fail-2))
           med=np.median(f); limit=max(50*med, 5*np.max(f), 50/len(f)+1e-12)
           if g.max()>limit: blend=max(blend,0.5)
           if blend>0:
              g = (1-blend)*g + blend*f; g=_normalize(g)
           f_try,M_try,c_try = _best_mix(f,g,M)
           if f_try is not None and M_try < M - 1e-15:
               f=f_try; M=M_try; c=c_try; best update; fail=0; if improvement large q=max(80,int(q*0.8)); continue
       fail+=1
       q=int(min(700, q*1.4 + 40))
       # if no time for next solve break if remaining<2
   return best_f,best_M
```

Detailed code:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import linprog

def _normalize(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if f.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    np.maximum(f, 0.0, out=f)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(f.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(f.size)
    f /= s
    return f
```
If f size 0, but we avoid.

`_autocorr`:
```
def _autocorr(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0)
    size = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)
    c = c[:2*n - 1]
    # remove tiny imaginary? irfft real.
    if c.min() < 0.0:
        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c
```
`(2*n-1).bit_length()` for int. If n=1, 1.bit_length=1 -> size=2, okay. Could size=1? For length1, next pow2≥1 is1; but `1 << 1 =2` works. Fine.

`_score_cmax(cmax,n)`:
```
def _score_cmax(cmax,n): return float(2.0*n*cmax)
```

`_grad_top`:
```
def _grad_top(f, idx):
    n=f.size
    grad=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if len(idx)==0: return grad
    for k in idx:
        k=int(k)
        if k < 0 or k >= 2*n-1: continue
        if k < n - 1:
            grad[0:k+1] += f[k::-1]
        else:
            left = k - (n - 1)
            if left <= 0:
                grad[0:k+1] += f[k::-1]
            else:
                right = k
                grad[left:right+1] += f[right:left-1:-1]
    grad *= (2.0 / float(len(idx)))
    return grad
```
For k=n-1, left=0, `grad[0:n] += f[n-1::-1]`. Good. For k=0, k<n-1, grad[0]+=f[0]. Good.

LP direction:
```
def _lp_direction(f, q, time_limit, rng=None):
    n=f.size
    c=_autocorr(f)
    M=float(c.max())
    if M <= 0 or not np.isfinite(M): return None,None
    L=2*n-1
    q=int(q)
    if q <=0: q=1
    if q >= L:
       idx=np.arange(L)
    else:
       sc=c
       if rng is not None:
          sc = c + rng.normal(0.0, 1e-8*M, L)
       idx=np.argpartition(sc, -q)[-q:]
    b=c[idx]
    invM=1.0/M
    fs=f*invM
    b_ub=b*invM
    A_ub=np.zeros((q, n+1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_ub[:, n] = -1.0
    for r,k in enumerate(idx):
       k=int(k)
       if k < n-1:
           A_ub[r, 0:k+1] = fs[k::-1]
       else:
           left = k - (n - 1)
           if left <=0:
               A_ub[r, 0:k+1] = fs[k::-1]
           else:
               right=k
               A_ub[r, left:right+1] = fs[right:left-1:-1]
    cvec=np.zeros(n+1); cvec[n]=1.0
    A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
    b_eq=np.ones(1)
    bounds=[(0.0,None) for _ in range(n)] + [(-1.0,1.0)]
    try:
        res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': float(time_limit)})
    except Exception:
        return None,None
    if res is None or not getattr(res,'success',False):
       return None,None
    x=res.x
    if x is None or x.size != n+1: return None,None
    g=x[:n].astype(np.float64,copy=True)
    t=float(x[n])
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)): return None, t
    np.maximum(g,0.0,out=g)
    s=float(g.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12: return None,t
    g /= s
    return g,t
```
Potential: If `rng.normal(0.0, 1e-8*M, L)` creates new array; if M very small, std 0; okay. `argpartition` with tie and noise. If q around L, okay.

`_best_mix`:
```
def _best_mix(f,g,M):
    n=f.size
    best_M=M
    best_f=None
    best_c=None
    alphas=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8)
    for alpha in alphas:
       f_try=(1.0-alpha)*f + alpha*g
       np.maximum(f_try,0.0,out=f_try)
       s=float(f_try.sum())
       if s <= 1e-15: continue
       f_try /= s
       c_try=_autocorr(f_try)
       M_try=float(c_try.max())
       if M_try < best_M - 1e-16:
          best_M=M_try; best_f=f_try; best_c=c_try
    return best_f,best_M,best_c
```
If q small, α=0.8 maybe bad; but all evaluated. 7 conv. Could reorder small first? We just compare. Good.

`_lp_block`:
```
def _lp_block(f, deadline, rng):
    f=_normalize(f)
    c=_autocorr(f)
    M=float(c.max())
    best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
    q=100
    fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        if fail > 10:
            break
        remaining=deadline - time.monotonic()
        tlim=min(6.0, max(1.0, 0.25*remaining))
        if tlim < 0.5: break
        g,t = _lp_direction(f, q, tlim, rng)
        if g is not None and t < -1e-10:
            blend=0.0
            if fail >= 2:
                blend=min(0.55, 0.15 + 0.1*(fail-2))
            med=float(np.median(f))
            limit=max(50.0*med, 5.0*float(np.max(f)), 50.0/f.size + 1e-12)
            if g.max() > limit:
                blend=max(blend, 0.5)
            if blend > 0.0:
                g = (1.0-blend)*g + blend*f
                g=_normalize(g)
            f_try,M_try,c_try = _best_mix(f,g,M)
            if f_try is not None and M_try < M - 1e-15:
                f=f_try; M=M_try; c=c_try
                if M < best_M - 1e-16:
                    best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
                fail=0
                # if progress was good, maybe reduce q to explore? Keep.
                if M < best_M? (best updated). Use oldM before.
                continue
        fail += 1
        q = int(min(700, int(q*1.4) + 50))
    if M < best_M - 1e-16:
        best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
    return best_f,best_M
```
Need capture oldM before accepting to adjust q? Not needed. If no improvement and q grows, after 700 and fail maybe many; break after fail>10. Good. Print improvement? Could print if improvement large:
```
if best_M < old_best_M - 1e-12: print(...)
```
In block, no verbose. We can pass `verbose=False`? Not.

`_mirror_block` code refine:
```
def _mirror_block(f, deadline, rng):
    f=_normalize(f)
    c=_autocorr(f)
    M=float(c.max())
    best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
    L=2*len(f)-1
    q=60
    if q>=L: q=max(1,L)
    eta=1.0
    fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        # pick q sometimes
        if fail > 6:
            q = int(min(250, q + 25))
        qq = min(q, L)
        if qq >= L:
            idx = np.arange(L)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -qq)[-qq:]
        grad = _grad_top(f, idx)
        gmax = float(grad.max())
        if gmax <= 1e-15:
            break
        step=eta
        accepted=False
        for _ in range(6):
            z = np.log(f + 1e-300) - (step * grad / gmax) # recomputes log each attempt, high cost. Better maintain logf.
```

`_mirror_block` full:
```
def _mirror_block(f, deadline, rng):
    f=_normalize(f)
    c=_autocorr(f); M=float(c.max())
    best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
    L=2*f.size-1
    q=60
    if q>=L: q=L
    eta=1.0
    fail=0
    logf=np.log(f+1e-300)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
       if fail > 6: q=int(min(250, q+25))
       qq=min(q,L)
       if qq>=L: idx=np.arange(L)
       else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -qq)[-qq:]
       grad=_grad_top(f, idx)
       gmax=float(grad.max())
       if gmax <= 1e-15: break
       step=eta
       accepted=False
       for _ in range(6):
          z=logf - step*grad/gmax
          zmax=float(z.max())
          if not np.isfinite(zmax): break
          z -= zmax
          f_try=np.exp(z)
          s=float(f_try.sum())
          if s <= 1e-300:
             step *= 0.5
             continue
          f_try /= s
          c_try=_autocorr(f_try)
          M_try=float(c_try.max())
          if M_try < M - 1e-16:
             f=f_try; c=c_try; M=M_try; logf=np.log(f+1e-300)
             eta=min(2.0, step*1.25)
             fail=0
             accepted=True
             if M < best_M - 1e-16:
                best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
             break
          step *= 0.5
          if step < 1e-4: break
       if accepted:
          continue
       fail +=1
       eta *= 0.6
       if fail > 12:
          sigma = 0.02 + 0.01*min(5, (fail-12)//5)
          noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, f.size)
          f_try = best_f * np.exp(noise)
          f_try = np.maximum(f_try,0.0)
          s=float(f_try.sum())
          if s <= 1e-15: break
          f_try /= s
          f=f_try; c=_autocorr(f); M=float(c.max()); logf=np.log(f+1e-300)
          fail=0; eta=1.0
          q=int(min(250, max(40, q//2)))
          if M < best_M - 1e-16:
             best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
       if fail > 30:
          break
    if M < best_M - 1e-16:
       best_M=M; best_f=f.copy()
    return best_f,best_M
```
If perturbation worsens M, subsequent mirror may try to recover; block_best remains best. Good. But if current f is much worse, LP? In `_improve_candidate`, after mirror if M worse than best, we may keep f (perturbed) to continue (exploration). But block returns best_f. In `_improve_candidate`, if M_mir < best_score update; for current f, it uses block output? If block returns best_f even if perturbation good. Then next LP starts from best, not exploration. If we want use perturbed for exploration, block should return both. Simpler: `_improve_candidate` can re-perturb on stagnation itself and pass to next block. It currently does. The block returns best; that's fine.

In `_improve_candidate`, after each block, if block output not improved global but is better than current, set current. For exploration, if stagnation, perturb best. Good.

`_improve_candidate` code:

def _improve_candidate(f, deadline, rng):
    f=_normalize(f)
    c=_autocorr(f); M=float(c.max())
    n=f.size
    best_f=f.copy(); best_M=M
    last_imp=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
        # if stuck, perturb
        if time.monotonic() - last_imp > 120.0:
            sigma = 0.03
            f_try = best_f * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
            f_try=_normalize(f_try)
            M_try=float(_autocorr(f_try).max())
            f=f_try; M=M_try
        # LP block
        lp_end = min(time.monotonic() + 60.0, deadline - 20.0)
        f_lp, M_lp = _lp_block(f, lp_end, rng)
        if M_lp < M:
            f=f_lp; M=M_lp
        if M_lp < best_M - 1e-16:
            best_M=M_lp; best_f=f_lp.copy(); last_imp=time.monotonic()
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0: break
        # mirror block
        mir_end=min(time.monotonic()+90.0, deadline-20.0)
        f_mir,M_mir=_mirror_block(f, mir_end, rng)
        if M_mir < M:
            f=f_mir; M=M_mir
        if M_mir < best_M - 1e-16:
            best_M=M_mir; best_f=f_mir.copy(); last_imp=time.monotonic()
    if M < best_M -1e-16: best...
    return best_f, float(2*n*best_M)

def _maybe_downsample(arr):
   m=arr.size
   if m <= 10000: return arr
   target=8192
   block=m//target
   if block <=1:
      return arr[:target]
   usable=target*block
   return arr[:usable].reshape(target, block).mean(axis=1)
```

if M_lp < M: f=f_lp; M=M_lp
if M_lp < best_M - tol: best...
```
If M_lp is best_M from block (could be original M if no improve), M_lp<M maybe if block improved current but not global. Good.

In `_mirror_block`, same.

Now, in propose, if top starts include arrays that are views? `_make_starts` uses `.copy` for base, sym normalized returns copy? `_normalize` returns new array. Good. When sorting, arrays in tuples. No issue.

Now, final scale to sum 10: If best_f contains tiny floor + positive; sum maybe1. Multiply. Use `arr = 10.0 * arr / s`. Then clip to 1000. Then final list. If after clipping sum changes? max not exceed anyway. If floating underflow? values ~0.001, fine.

Now, if `budget_s` less than 30, deadline = start+30? maybe still search. Not important.

Let's produce final code.
</think>

```python
"""
Step-function optimizer for minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The program uses a hybrid strategy:
  * several diverse smoothed/sparse starting points,
  * active-set linear-programming directions that minimize the first-order
    maximum convolution over the most relevant lags,
  * multiplicative mirror-descent polishing with top-lag gradients,
  * careful line search and periodic low-amplitude perturbations.

It returns the best non-negative sequence found before the deadline.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import linprog


def _normalize(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if f.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    np.maximum(f, 0.0, out=f)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(f.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(f.size)
    f /= s
    return f


def _autocorr(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)[:2 * n - 1]
    if c.min() < 0.0:
        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def _score_from_cmax(cmax, n):
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax)


def _grad_top(f, idx):
    n = f.size
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if len(idx) == 0:
        return grad
    m = len(idx)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    for k in idx:
        k = int(k)
        if k < 0 or k >= L:
            continue
        if k < n - 1:
            grad[0:k + 1] += f[k::-1]
        else:
            left = k - (n - 1)
            if left <= 0:
                grad[0:n] += f[n - 1::-1]
            else:
                grad[left:n] += f[n - 1:left - 1:-1]
    grad *= (2.0 / float(m))
    return grad


def _lp_direction(f, q, time_limit, rng=None):
    n = f.size
    c = _autocorr(f)
    M = float(c.max())
    if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None, None
    L = 2 * n - 1
    q = int(q)
    if q <= 0:
        q = 1
    if q >= L:
        top = np.arange(L)
    else:
        sc = c
        if rng is not None:
            sc = c + rng.normal(0.0, 1e-7 * M, L)
        top = np.argpartition(sc, -q)[-q:]

    idx = top
    thr = M - max(0.005 * M, 1e-12)
    near = np.where(c >= thr)[0]
    if 0 < len(near) <= q:
        merged = np.unique(np.concatenate((near, top)))
        if merged.size > q:
            merged = merged[np.argpartition(c[merged], -q)[-q:]]
        idx = merged

    invM = 1.0 / M
    fs = f * invM
    q = int(len(idx))

    A_ub = np.zeros((q, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_ub[:, n] = -1.0

    for r, k in enumerate(idx):
        k = int(k)
        if k < n - 1:
            A_ub[r, 0:k + 1] = fs[k::-1]
        else:
            left = k - (n - 1)
            if left <= 0:
                A_ub[r, 0:n] = fs[n - 1::-1]
            else:
                A_ub[r, left:n] = fs[n - 1:left - 1:-1]

    b_ub = np.ones(q, dtype=np.float64)
    cvec = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cvec[n] = 1.0
    A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
    b_eq = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None) for _ in range(n)] + [(-1.0, 1.0)]

    try:
        res = linprog(
            cvec,
            A_ub=A_ub,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            A_eq=A_eq,
            b_eq=b_eq,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"time_limit": float(time_limit)},
        )
    except Exception:
        return None, None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None, None
    x = res.x
    if x is None or x.size != n + 1:
        return None, None

    g = np.asarray(x[:n], dtype=np.float64).copy()
    t = float(x[n])
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return None, t
    np.maximum(g, 0.0, out=g)
    s = float(g.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None, t
    g /= s
    return g, t


def _best_mix(f, g, M):
    best_M = M
    best_f = None
    best_c = None
    alphas = (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.55, 0.80)
    for alpha in alphas:
        f_try = (1.0 - alpha) * f + alpha * g
        np.maximum(f_try, 0.0, out=f_try)
        s = float(f_try.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            continue
        f_try /= s
        c_try = _autocorr(f_try)
        M_try = float(c_try.max())
        if M_try < best_M - 1e-16:
            best_M = M_try
            best_f = f_try
            best_c = c_try
    return best_f, best_M, best_c


def _lp_block(f, deadline, rng):
    f = _normalize(f)
    floor = 1e-8 / f.size
    f = f + floor
    f = _normalize(f)

    c = _autocorr(f)
    M = float(c.max())
    best_f = f.copy()
    best_M = M

    q = 200
    max_q = 1000
    fail = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        if fail > 10:
            break
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        tlim = min(6.0, max(0.8, 0.25 * remaining))
        if tlim < 0.5:
            break

        g, t = _lp_direction(f, q, tlim, rng)
        if g is not None and t < -1e-10:
            blend = 0.0
            if fail >= 2:
                blend = min(0.55, 0.15 + 0.10 * (fail - 2))
            med = float(np.median(f))
            limit = max(50.0 * med, 5.0 * float(np.max(f)), 50.0 / f.size + 1e-12)
            if g.max() > limit:
                blend = max(blend, 0.50)
            if blend > 0.0:
                g = (1.0 - blend) * g + blend * f
                g = _normalize(g)

            f_try, M_try, c_try = _best_mix(f, g, M)
            if f_try is not None and M_try < M - 1e-15:
                f = f_try
                M = M_try
                c = c_try
                if M < best_M - 1e-16:
                    best_M = M
                    best_f = f.copy()
                fail = 0
                if M < best_M? :
                    pass
                continue

        fail += 1
        q = int(min(max_q, int(q * 1.4) + 50))

    if M < best_M - 1e-16:
        best_M = M
        best_f = f.copy()
    return best_f, best_M


def _mirror_block(f, deadline, rng):
    f = _normalize(f)
    c = _autocorr(f)
    M = float(c.max())
    n = f.size
    L = 2 * n - 1

    best_f = f.copy()
    best_M = M

    q = 60
    if q >= L:
        q = max(1, L)
    max_q = 260

    eta = 1.0
    fail = 0
    logf = np.log(f + 1e-300)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        if fail > 6:
            q = int(min(max_q, q + 25))
        qq = min(q, L)

        thr = M - max(0.02 * M, 1e-12)
        near = np.where(c >= thr)[0]
        if 0 < len(near) <= qq:
            idx = near
        elif qq >= L:
            idx = np.arange(L)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -qq)[-qq:]

        grad = _grad_top(f, idx)
        gmax = float(grad.max())
        if gmax <= 1e-15:
            break

        step = eta
        accepted = False

        for _ in range(6):
            z = logf - step * grad / gmax
            zmax = float(z.max())
            if not np.isfinite(zmax):
                break
            z -= zmax
            f_try = np.exp(z)
            s = float(f_try.sum())
            if s <= 1e-300:
                step *= 0.5
                continue
            f_try /= s
            c_try = _autocorr(f_try)
            M_try = float(c_try.max())

            if M_try < M - 1e-16:
                f = f_try
                c = c_try
                M = M_try
                logf = np.log(f + 1e-300)
                eta = min(2.0, step * 1.25)
                fail = 0
                accepted = True
                if M < best_M - 1e-16:
                    best_M = M
                    best_f = f.copy()
                break

            step *= 0.5
            if step < 1e-4:
                break

        if accepted:
            continue

        fail += 1
        eta *= 0.6

        if fail > 12:
            sigma = 0.02 + 0.01 * min(5, (fail - 12) // 5)
            noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
            f_try = best_f * np.exp(noise)
            f_try = np.maximum(f_try, 0.0)
            s = float(f_try.sum())
            if s <= 1e-15:
                break
            f_try /= s
            f = f_try
            c = _autocorr(f)
            M = float(c.max())
            logf = np.log(f + 1e-300)
            fail = 0
            eta = 1.0
            q = int(min(max_q, max(40, q // 2)))
            if M < best_M - 1e-16:
                best_M = M
                best_f = f.copy()

        if fail > 30:
            break

    if M < best_M - 1e-16:
        best_M = M
        best_f = f.copy()
    return best_f, best_M


def _improve_candidate(f, deadline, rng):
    f = _normalize(f)
    c = _autocorr(f)
    M = float(c.max())
    n = f.size

    best_f = f.copy()
    best_M = M
    last_imp = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        if time.monotonic() - last_imp > 120.0:
            sigma = 0.03
            f_try = best_f * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
            f_try = _normalize(f_try)
            f = f_try
            M = float(_autocorr(f).max())

        lp_end = min(time.monotonic() + 45.0, deadline - 25.0)
        f_lp, M_lp = _lp_block(f, lp_end, rng)
        if M_lp < M:
            f = f_lp
            M = M_lp
        if M_lp < best_M - 1e-16:
            best_M = M_lp
            best_f = f_lp.copy()
            last_imp = time.monotonic()

        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 35.0:
            break

        mir_end = min(time.monotonic() + 75.0, deadline - 25.0)
        f_mir, M_mir = _mirror_block(f, mir_end, rng)
        if M_mir < M:
            f = f_mir
            M = M_mir
        if M_mir < best_M - 1e-16:
            best_M = M_mir
            best_f = f_mir.copy()
            last_imp = time.monotonic()

    if M < best_M - 1e-16:
        best_M = M
        best_f = f.copy()

    return best_f, _score_from_cmax(best_M, n)


def _make_starts(base, n, rng):
    base = _normalize(base)
    starts = []

    starts.append(base.copy())

    rev = base[::-1].copy()
    starts.append(rev)

    sym = 0.5 * (base + rev)
    starts.append(_normalize(sym))

    for sigma in (0.01, 0.02, 0.04, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25):
        f = base * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
        starts.append(_normalize(f))

    if n <= 12000:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

        for A in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 50.0):
            f = 1.0 + A * (2.0 * x - 1.0) ** 2
            starts.append(_normalize(f))

        for A in (1.0, 3.0, 10.0, 30.0):
            f = 1.0 / (1.0 + A * (x - 0.5) ** 2)
            starts.append(_normalize(f))

        for c1, c2 in ((0.2, 0.8), (0.3, 0.7), (0.1, 0.9)):
            for w in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
                f = (
                    np.exp(-((x - c1) / w) ** 2)
                    + np.exp(-((x - c2) / w) ** 2)
                    + 1e-6
                )
                starts.append(_normalize(f))

        for _ in range(10):
            f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            for k in (2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12):
                amp = 0.1 * rng.uniform(0.1, 1.0)
                ph = 2.0 * np.pi * rng.random()
                f = f * (1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * x + ph))
            f = np.maximum(f, 0.0) + 1e-6
            starts.append(_normalize(f))

    if len(starts) > 60:
        starts = starts[:60]
    return starts


def _maybe_downsample(arr):
    m = arr.size
    if m <= 10000:
        return arr
    target = 8192
    block = m // target
    if block <= 1:
        return arr[:target]
    usable = target * block
    return arr[:usable].reshape(target, block).mean(axis=1)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(30.0, budget_s - 30.0)

    try:
        base = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        base = np.ones(6121, dtype=np.float64)

    if base.size == 0:
        base = np.ones(6121, dtype=np.float64)

    base = np.nan_to_num(base, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    base = _maybe_downsample(np.clip(base, 0.0, 1000.0))
    base = _normalize(base)
    n = base.size

    print(f"Working length: {n}")

    starts = _make_starts(base, n, rng)
    scored = []
    for i, f in enumerate(starts):
        f = _normalize(f)
        cmax = float(_autocorr(f).max())
        sc = _score_from_cmax(cmax, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, i, f))

    if not scored:
        scored.append((2.0, 0, base.copy()))

    scored.sort()
    best_score = float(scored[0][0])
    best_f = np.asarray(scored[0][2], dtype=np.float64)
    best_f = _normalize(best_f)

    print(f"Initial best score: {best_score:.10f}")

    top = []
    for sc, idx_tmp, f in scored:
        if len(top) >= 6:
            break
        if sc <= best_score + 0.05:
            top.append((float(sc), np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)))

    allocations = (0.85, 0.10, 0.05)
    for j in range(min(len(top), len(allocations))):
        start_score, f_start = top[j]
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60.0:
            break
        if j > 0 and start_score > best_score + 0.02:
            continue

        remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 60.0)
        alloc = remaining * allocations[j]
        if alloc < 25.0 and j > 0:
            continue
        if alloc < 20.0:
            break

        end = time.monotonic() + alloc
        if end > deadline - 30.0:
            end = deadline - 30.0
        if end - time.monotonic() < 20.0:
            continue

        print(f"\nRestart {j}: initial {start_score:.10f}, budget {alloc:.1f}s")
        f_try, sc_try = _improve_candidate(f_start, end, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_try)
            best_f = _normalize(f_try)
            print(f"  improvement: {best_score:.10f}")

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 35.0:
        end = min(time.monotonic() + 120.0, deadline - 10.0)
        f_try, sc_try = _mirror_block(best_f, end, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_try)
            best_f = _normalize(f_try)

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
        end = min(time.monotonic() + 90.0, deadline - 5.0)
        f_try, sc_try = _lp_block(best_f, end, rng)
        if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_try)
            best_f = _normalize(f_try)

    best_f = _normalize(best_f)

    arr = np.asarray(best_f, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(arr.sum())
    arr = (10.0 * arr) / s
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    final_list = [float(x) for x in arr]

    final_score = np.inf
    try:
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(final_list))
    except Exception:
        final_score = best_score

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        fallback = _normalize(base)
        fallback = (10.0 * fallback) / max(float(fallback.sum()), 1e-300)
        final_list = [float(x) for x in fallback]
        try:
            final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(final_list))
        except Exception:
            final_score = 2.0

    print(f"Final returned score: {final_score:.10f}")
    return final_list
```
